Polymers are retained because they are identified as polymers. Performance, purchase status and missing card fields do not decide retention.

Synthesis text

Reported in the supplied text

  • PTMG-HDI was synthesized via condensation of PTMG with HDI.

    Original evidence (1 source passage)

    1. main · Methods / Preparation of PTMG-HDI, paragraph 25 · chunk src-0e0464a320ed4ac7c4a6

      PTMG-HDI was synthesized via condensation of PTMG with HDI.

      Quote matched to converted source text

      Source XML converted with preserved superscripts/subscripts (jats_script_notation_v1).

      Source SHA-256: ffc5376b77b6b81e29c52134d1f96d0185752dd35d35cb48172b661b13c8e1a8

  • Briefly, 2.5 g (1.25 mmol) of PTMG and 250 μL (1.5 mmol) of HDI were dissolved in 5 mL of CH₂Cl₂. 5 μL of DBTDL was added to the solution as a reaction catalyst. The final molar ratio of the monomers (PTMG:HDI) was 1:1.2. The mixture was stirred at 40 °C for 3 h in a N₂-filled glove box. Then, 5 mL of DMAc was added to the reaction mixture every 2 h 3 times repeated 3 times, and stirred for 12 h. Finally, a transparent PTMG-HDI solution was obtained.

    Tested/prepared specimen: PTMG-HDI solution

    Original evidence (1 source passage)

    1. main · Methods / Preparation of PTMG-HDI, paragraph 25 · chunk src-0e0464a320ed4ac7c4a6

      Briefly, 2.5 g (1.25 mmol) of PTMG and 250 μL (1.5 mmol) of HDI were dissolved in 5 mL of CH₂Cl₂. 5 μL of DBTDL was added to the solution as a reaction catalyst. The final molar ratio of the monomers (PTMG:HDI) was 1:1.2. The mixture was stirred at 40 °C for 3 h in a N₂-filled glove box. Then, 5 mL of DMAc was added to the reaction mixture every 2 h 3 times repeated 3 times, and stirred for 12 h. Finally, a transparent PTMG-HDI solution was obtained.

      Quote matched to converted source text

      Source XML converted with preserved superscripts/subscripts (jats_script_notation_v1).

      Source SHA-256: ffc5376b77b6b81e29c52134d1f96d0185752dd35d35cb48172b661b13c8e1a8

  • The PTMG-HDI and PTMG-HDI-BHDS polymer solutions were mixed with LiFSI in dimethylacetamide (DMAc) at a mass ratio of 2:1 (polymer: LiFSI) and stirred for 3 h. These viscous solutions were degassed under vacuum, cast into a Teflon mold, and then dried in a vacuum oven at 65 °C for 48 h to obtain the PEO/LiFSI, PTMG-HDI/LiFSI, and PTMG-HDI-BHDS/LiFSI electrolytes.

    Tested/prepared specimen: PTMG-HDI/LiFSI

    Original evidence (1 source passage)

    1. main · Methods / Preparation of the PEO/LiFSI, PTMG-HDI/LiFSI, and PTMG-HDI-BHDS/LiFSI electrolytes, paragraph 27 · chunk src-6524f9582cedc0d6c138

      The PTMG-HDI and PTMG-HDI-BHDS polymer solutions were mixed with LiFSI in dimethylacetamide (DMAc) at a mass ratio of 2:1 (polymer: LiFSI) and stirred for 3 h. These viscous solutions were degassed under vacuum, cast into a Teflon mold, and then dried in a vacuum oven at 65 °C for 48 h to obtain the PEO/LiFSI, PTMG-HDI/LiFSI, and PTMG-HDI-BHDS/LiFSI electrolytes.

      Quote matched to converted source text

      Source XML converted with preserved superscripts/subscripts (jats_script_notation_v1).

Assistant source-review notes — scientific checks remain
  • The BHDS feed molar ratio 1:2.4:1 is separate from the polymer:salt mass ratio 2:1.
  • BHDS molecular weight 7.3 × 10⁵ and viscosity 1.3 × 10⁴ preserve script notation. The 88.3 MPa film strength is separate from LiFSI-mixture conductivity 2.4 × 10⁻⁴ S cm⁻¹ at 30°C.

  • Briefly, 2.5 g (1.25 mmol) of PTMG, 500 μL (3.0 mmol) of HDI, and 5 μL of DBTDL were dissolved in 5 mL of CH₂Cl₂ and stirred at 40 °C for 1 h in a N₂-filled glove box, and then 200 μL (1.25 mmol) of BHDS was added to the above solution. The final molar ratio of the monomers (PTMG:HDI:BHDS) was 1:2.4:1. Subsequently, 5 mL of DMAc was added into the reaction mixture every 2 h and repeated 3 times, and stirred for 12 h to obtain the PTMG-HDI-BHDS solution.

    Tested/prepared specimen: PTMG-HDI-BHDS solution

    Original evidence (1 source passage)

    1. main · Methods / Preparation of PTMG-HDI-BHDS, paragraph 26 · chunk src-d1659b2c731d650cec05

      Briefly, 2.5 g (1.25 mmol) of PTMG, 500 μL (3.0 mmol) of HDI, and 5 μL of DBTDL were dissolved in 5 mL of CH₂Cl₂ and stirred at 40 °C for 1 h in a N₂-filled glove box, and then 200 μL (1.25 mmol) of BHDS was added to the above solution. The final molar ratio of the monomers (PTMG:HDI:BHDS) was 1:2.4:1. Subsequently, 5 mL of DMAc was added into the reaction mixture every 2 h and repeated 3 times, and stirred for 12 h to obtain the PTMG-HDI-BHDS solution.

  • The PTMG-HDI and PTMG-HDI-BHDS polymer solutions were mixed with LiFSI in dimethylacetamide (DMAc) at a mass ratio of 2:1 (polymer: LiFSI) and stirred for 3 h. These viscous solutions were degassed under vacuum, cast into a Teflon mold, and then dried in a vacuum oven at 65 °C for 48 h to obtain the PEO/LiFSI, PTMG-HDI/LiFSI, and PTMG-HDI-BHDS/LiFSI electrolytes.

      The PTMG-HDI and PTMG-HDI-BHDS polymer solutions were mixed with LiFSI in dimethylacetamide (DMAc) at a mass ratio of 2:1 (polymer: LiFSI) and stirred for 3 h. These viscous solutions were degassed under vacuum, cast into a Teflon mold, and then dried in a vacuum oven at 65 °C for 48 h to obtain the PEO/LiFSI, PTMG-HDI/LiFSI, and PTMG-HDI-BHDS/LiFSI electrolytes.

      The PTMG-HDI and PTMG-HDI-BHDS polymer solutions were mixed with LiFSI in dimethylacetamide (DMAc) at a mass ratio of 2:1 (polymer: LiFSI) and stirred for 3 h. These viscous solutions were degassed under vacuum, cast into a Teflon mold, and then dried in a vacuum oven at 65 °C for 48 h to obtain the PEO/LiFSI, PTMG-HDI/LiFSI, and PTMG-HDI-BHDS/LiFSI electrolytes.

      Quote matched to converted source text

Synthesis text

Reported in the supplied text

  • SPAN was synthesized according to a modified method previously reported⁶¹. A total of 1.0 g of polyacrylonitrile (PAN) and 3.5 g of S powder were uniformly mixed in 10 mL of ethanol by high-energy ball milling for 4 h. Then, SPAN with a sulfur content of 45.0 wt% was obtained after heating the composite at 300 °C for 450 min in a N₂ atmosphere.

P(MMA-BA-(F-MA)) copolymer

Synthesis text

Reported in the supplied text

  • The P(MMA-BA-(F-MA)) copolymer was prepared by emulsion polymerization.

    Original evidence (1 source passage)

    1. main · 2. Materials and Methods / 2.3. Preparation of the Self-Healing Polymer Precursor, paragraph 9 · chunk src-189865c0a792b53247cf

      The P(MMA-BA-(F-MA)) copolymer was prepared by emulsion polymerization.

      Quote matched to converted source text

      Source XML converted with preserved superscripts/subscripts (jats_script_notation_v1).

      Source SHA-256: cfd69ea12c067601a053396289614766ecd9d617fe86497483e61c116407fadc

  • The mixture (MMA:BMA:F-MA = 1:2:1 (in molar ratio)) was added to the above solution under vigorous stirring for 1 h to form an emulsified solution. After adding ammonium persulfate (as an initiator) and stirring continuously for 6 h, the resulting emulsion was poured into 3 wt% Al₂(SO₄)₃ solution to yield a precipitate that was isolated by filtration and washed with hot deionized water (55 °C) in order to remove any impurities such as residual monomers and emulsifier. The P(MMA-BA-(F-MA)) copolymer was finally obtained by drying the purified precipitate in a vacuum oven at 60 °C for 12 h and kept in a desiccator for membrane preparation.

    Original evidence (1 source passage)

    1. main · 2. Materials and Methods / 2.3. Preparation of the Self-Healing Polymer Precursor, paragraph 9 · chunk src-189865c0a792b53247cf

      The mixture (MMA:BMA:F-MA = 1:2:1 (in molar ratio)) was added to the above solution under vigorous stirring for 1 h to form an emulsified solution. After adding ammonium persulfate (as an initiator) and stirring continuously for 6 h, the resulting emulsion was poured into 3 wt% Al₂(SO₄)₃ solution to yield a precipitate that was isolated by filtration and washed with hot deionized water (55 °C) in order to remove any impurities such as residual monomers and emulsifier. The P(MMA-BA-(F-MA)) copolymer was finally obtained by drying the purified precipitate in a vacuum oven at 60 °C for 12 h and kept in a desiccator for membrane preparation.

      Quote matched to converted source text

      Source XML converted with preserved superscripts/subscripts (jats_script_notation_v1).

      Source SHA-256: cfd69ea12c067601a053396289614766ecd9d617fe86497483e61c116407fadc

Assistant source-review notes — scientific checks remain
  • The shared membrane specimen is too broad for network mechanical, thermal, thin-film and wetted observations.
  • The Methods 12 μm thickness and Results approximately 15 μm statement still need an explicit conflict record.
  • The room-temperature conductivity observation preserves 10 μL added liquid electrolyte, separate from the temperature series. Wet/dry context is still incomplete.
  • Repair at 80°C under nitrogen for about two hours before cell assembly is not evidence of autonomous repair inside an operating cell.
  • Several bare numbers lost approximation/limit qualifiers and are withheld by the final validator rather than presented as precise measurements.

Synthesis text

Reported in the supplied text

  • Initially, a homogeneous solution of copolymer and lithium salt was prepared by mixing the components at 50 °C for 1 h. Subsequently, crosslinking agent was added to the mixture, after complete dissolution, the resulting viscous solution was cast with a doctor blade onto a glass plate, and then dried in vacuum at 90 °C for 24 h; finally, self-healing polymer electrolyte membrane was obtained.

    Tested/prepared specimen: SHPEM

    Original evidence (1 source passage)

      Initially, a homogeneous solution of copolymer and lithium salt was prepared by mixing the components at 50 °C for 1 h. Subsequently, crosslinking agent was added to the mixture, after complete dissolution, the resulting viscous solution was cast with a doctor blade onto a glass plate, and then dried in vacuum at 90 °C for 24 h; finally, self-healing polymer electrolyte membrane was obtained.

      Quote matched to converted source text

Synthesis text

Reported in the supplied text

  • A series of polymers were synthesized through a two-step polycondensation reaction. To synthesize a bis-isocyanate-terminated preoligomer, α, ω-dihydroxyethylpropoxyl-PDMS (M_{w} = 4,600 g mol⁻¹, as a soft segment diol) was reacted with isophorone diisocyanate (IPDI) monomer (M_{(PDMS)}/M_{(IPDI)} = 1/2) in N, N^{′}-dimethylacetamide (DMAc) using dibutyltin dilaurate (DBTDL) as a catalyst. Then, 4,4^{′}-dithiodianiline (SS) (an aromatic disulfide) and/or 4,4^{′}-bis(hydroxymethyl)-2,2^{′}-bipyridine (BNB) were added to the solution as a chain extender to complete the PDMS–SS–IP–BNB synthesis.

    Original evidence (1 source passage)

    1. main · Results, paragraph 8 · chunk src-08d3510e5447dda4de4b

      A series of polymers were synthesized through a two-step polycondensation reaction. To synthesize a bis-isocyanate-terminated preoligomer, α, ω-dihydroxyethylpropoxyl-PDMS (M_{w} = 4,600 g mol⁻¹, as a soft segment diol) was reacted with isophorone diisocyanate (IPDI) monomer (M_{(PDMS)}/M_{(IPDI)} = 1/2) in N, N^{′}-dimethylacetamide (DMAc) using dibutyltin dilaurate (DBTDL) as a catalyst. Then, 4,4^{′}-dithiodianiline (SS) (an aromatic disulfide) and/or 4,4^{′}-bis(hydroxymethyl)-2,2^{′}-bipyridine (BNB) were added to the solution as a chain extender to complete the PDMS–SS–IP–BNB synthesis.

      Quote matched to converted source text

      Source XML converted with preserved superscripts/subscripts (jats_script_notation_v1).

      Source SHA-256: b6e614f9b777ddcb51109ddfa334540b3cf9a0acd27d62cfa80d29caea3a6bd4

        "synthesis_text": {
          "status": "reported",
          "items": [
            {
              "entity_id": "p5",
              "value": "A typical polymerization procedure for P3 is described below. HO–PDMS–OH (23.0 g, 5.0 mmol) in a dried glass vessel equipped with a mechanical stirrer was heated in an oil bath at 100 °C under vacuum (<133 Pa) for 1 h to remove any moisture, and then cooled to 70 °C. IPDI (2.44 g, 11 mmol) and DBTDL (0.08 g) were dissolved in DMAc (20 mL), dropwise added into the vessel, and stirred for 3 h under a N₂ atmosphere. After the synthesis of the pre-polymer, 4,4^{′}-dithiodianiline (0.6 g, 2.5 mmol) and 4, 4^{′}-bis(hydroxymethyl)-2,2^{′}-bipyridine (0.465 g, 2.5 mmol) in 6 mL of dry DMAc were added to the pre-polymer solution. After stirring at 70 °C for another 3 h, the solution was added by methanol (3 mL), and further stirred for 30 min to ensure the consumption enough of isocyanate groups. Then, the solution was poured into a Poly tetra fluoroethylene (PTFE) plate that was subsequently put in a vacuum oven at 90 °C for 12 h to allow the reaction to complete. By dissolving the crude polymer in 40 mL of CH₂Cl₂ and then precipitating it into petroleum ether, we successfully obtained the purified P3 sample (16 g, yield 60%) as a yellow brown elastomer.",
              "specimen_id": null,
              "evidence": [
                {
                  "chunk_id": "src-abdf1ced28f9b1e9fb83",
                  "quote": "A typical polymerization procedure for P3 is described below. HO–PDMS–OH (23.0 g, 5.0 mmol) in a dried glass vessel equipped with a mechanical stirrer was heated in an oil bath at 100 °C under vacuum (<133 Pa) for 1 h to remove any moisture, and then cooled to 70 °C. IPDI (2.44 g, 11 mmol) and DBTDL (0.08 g) were dissolved in DMAc (20 mL), dropwise added into the vessel, and stirred for 3 h under a N₂ atmosphere. After the synthesis of the pre-polymer, 4,4^{′}-dithiodianiline (0.6 g, 2.5 mmol) and 4, 4^{′}-bis(hydroxymethyl)-2,2^{′}-bipyridine (0.465 g, 2.5 mmol) in 6 mL of dry DMAc were added to the pre-polymer solution. After stirring at 70 °C for another 3 h, the solution was added by methanol (3 mL), and further stirred for 30 min to ensure the consumption enough of isocyanate groups. Then, the solution was poured into a Poly tetra fluoroethylene (PTFE) plate that was subsequently put in a vacuum oven at 90 °C for 12 h to allow the reaction to complete. By dissolving the crude polymer in 40 mL of CH₂Cl₂ and then precipitating it into petroleum ether, we successfully obtained the purified P3 sample (16 g, yield 60%) as a yellow brown elastomer."
                }
              ]
